    Important Words
    • sva-janam — a dear one
      Compound: sva (one's own) + janam (person). 'One's own' is a contingent designation in saṁsāra — daivam can erase the relationship. Gauḍīyas contrast this with sambandha to Kṛṣṇa, which daiva cannot dissolve.
    • daivam — Providence, fate
      Often translated as fate, daivam in Bhāgavata usage is the inscrutable arrangement of the Lord — kṛṣṇa-līlā operating through karma. Not blind chance but His sweet will, even when it bruises.
    • adakṣiṇam — unfavourable
      Compound: a (not) + dakṣiṇam (favourable, southerly, generous). Dakṣiṇā is also the priest's fee — dakṣiṇa means rightward, auspicious. When daiva is left-handed, all relations turn cold.
    • na anusmaranti — do not remember
      Plural verb: na (not) + anu-√smṛ (to recall after). Forgetting is the universal failure mode of conditioned relations. Bhakti is the only relationship in which forgetfulness is impossible from His side.
    Friends and family members — even children, brothers and parents — forget a dear one whom Providence no longer favors.
    Purport

    Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī and Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura both comment that Kuntī does not blame her relatives for her suffering. Thus she calls them “most saintly persons” and alludes here to her own bad fortune as the cause of her unhappiness.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Friends and family members—even children, brothers and parents—forget a dear one whom Providence no longer favors.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Friends, family members, children, brothers and parents should remember a person with affection. The order is according to increasing affection.

    Related values & verses
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    When the wind shifts, even the smoke that hugged the fire forgets it. So with kin — when daiva turns away, the closest hearts grow distant. Kuntī states this as the world's law, not as accusation.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.20

    ↗
    శ్రీవసుదేవ ఉవాచ అమ్బ మాస్మానసూయేథా దైవక్రీడనకాన్ నరాన్ । ఈశస్య హి వశే లోక: కురుతే కార్యతేఽథ వా ॥ ౨౦ ॥
    śrī-vasudeva uvāca
    [shree-vasudeva uvaacha]
    amba māsmān asūyethā
    [amba maasmaan asooyethaa]
    daiva-krīḍanakān narān
    [daiva-kreedanakaan naraan]
    īśasya hi vaśe lokaḥ
    [eeshasya hi vashe lokah]
    kurute kāryate ’tha vā
    [kurute kaaryate ’tha vaa]
    Word-for-word
    śrī - vasudevaḥ uvāca — Śrī Vasudeva said amba — my dear sister mā — please do not asmān — with us asūyethāḥ — be angry daiva — of fate krīḍanakān — the playthings narān — men īśasya — of the Supreme Lord hi — indeed vaśe — under the control lokaḥ — a person kurute — acts on his own kāryate — is made to act by others atha vā — or else .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)

    Important Words
    • daiva-krīḍanakān — the playthings of fate
      Compound: daiva (Providence) + krīḍanakān (toys, accusative plural). Vasudeva names humanity as the Lord's playthings — līlā-vibhūti. Gauḍīyas read this with humility: the bhakta consents to be His toy.
    • īśasya — under the Supreme Lord's control
      Compound: īśasya (of the Lord, genitive) + vaśe (locative of vaśa, control). Theological centre-pillar — every act, whether self-initiated or other-impelled, falls within the Lord's vaśa. Sambandha begins here.
    • kurute kāryate — acts on his own or is made to act
      Active vs. causative passive of √kṛ. Both modes of action are equally under īśa-vaśa. Gauḍīyas align with this against any doctrine of autonomous free will — and against any doctrine of merely mechanical determinism. Bhagavān's vaśa is personal and loving.
    • amba — my dear sister (mother-form)
      Vocative of ambā — mother. Vasudeva addresses his sister with the maternal tenderness Vraja uses for Yaśodā. Tenderness softens the doctrinal correction he is about to deliver.
    Śrī Vasudeva said: Dear sister, please do not be angry with us. We are only ordinary men, playthings of fate. Indeed, whether a person acts on his own or is forced by others, he is always under the Supreme Lord’s control.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śrī Vasudeva said: Dear sister, please do not be angry with us. We are only ordinary men, playthings of fate. Indeed, whether a person acts on his own or is forced by others, he is always under the Supreme Lord's control.
    Commentary

    "O most dear one (amba), younger sister! Do not find fault in us who are like play things in the hands of fate. Under the control of the Lord, all people act with so-called independence, and are made to act without choice."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    O mother (amba)! He says this out of affection for her as a younger sister. Sometimes iśa-krīdāna-kātarān (men who suffer by the will of the Lord) is seen instead of daiva-krīḍanakam narān (men who are playthings of fate). As men (narān) we have little power compared to devatās and others. We are not independent. Do not hate us because (hi) all (atha) people who perform acts independently or have acts done to them by others are actually engaged in this under the control of the Supreme Lord. We are dependent. He makes even those who act perform their actions in that particular way. Citsukha has roga-jarā-maraṇādi dharmiṇo vā in the first line.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Discriminative WisdomAsteya (Non-stealing / Honesty)Kartavya (Duty / Responsibility)Tapas (Austerity / Discipline)

    BG 17.20 BG 2.60 BG 15.20 BG 2.47 BG 14.3 BG 12.1 SB 1.2BG 12.3 BG 4.8 BG 18.54 BG 17.4 BG 6.35 BG 7.1
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A chess-piece may resist or comply, but it is moved either way. Vasudeva tells Kuntī plainly: we are not the agents we imagine; the Lord's hand is on every move. The complaint is true but its addressee is wrong.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.21

    ↗
    కంసప్రతాపితా: సర్వే వయం యాతా దిశం దిశమ్ । ఏతర్హ్యేవ పున: స్థానం దైవేనాసాదితా: స్వస: ॥ ౨౧ ॥
    kaṁsa-pratāpitāḥ sarve

    vayaṁ yātā diśaṁ diśam
    [vayam yaataa disham disham]

    kaṁsa — by Kaṁsa pratāpitāḥ — severely troubled sarve — all vayam — we yātāḥ — went away diśam diśam — in various directions etarhi eva — just now punaḥ — again sthānam — to our proper places daivena — by Providence āsāditāḥ — brought svasaḥ — O sister .

    Important Words
    • kaṁsa-pratāpitāḥ — severely troubled by Kaṁsa
      Compound: kaṁsa (the tyrant of Mathurā) + pratāpitāḥ (heated, tormented). Pratāp — to scorch — captures the systemic terror Kaṁsa inflicted. Kṛṣṇa's appearance was precisely to relieve this.
    • diśam diśam — in various directions
      Repetition (āmreḍita) of diśam (direction, accusative). Sanskrit grammar uses doubling for distributive sense — 'each in his own direction.' The scattering was total.
    • daivena āsāditāḥ — brought by Providence
      Compound: daivena (instrumental of daiva) + āsāditāḥ (brought, attained). The same daiva that scattered them re-gathers them. Gauḍīyas read every chapter of life under one signature: His hand.
    • sthānam — proper places
      From √sthā — that which is stably one's own. The Yadus return not to addresses but to sthāna, their true station. For the bhakta, sthāna is finally Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet.
    Harassed by Kaṁsa, we all fled in various directions, but by the grace of Providence we have now finally been able to return to our homes, my dear sister.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Harassed by Kaṁsa, we all fled in various directions, but by the grace of Providence we have now finally been able to return to our homes, my dear sister.
    Commentary

    "We all fled away to various places being tormented by Kamsa." This statement refers to other Yadavas who had fled out of fear of Kamsa. (vasudeva was imprisoned so could not flee.) "O sister (svsah) in time (etarhi eva) we have returned to our places."

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    From our young days we Yadus were afflicted by evil Kaṁsa’s strength and later when he made allies with Keśī, Ariṣṭa, Tṛṇavarta, and Pūtanā we were more afflicted. But after Kaṁsa heard the message from the sky, he afflicted us even more. When Vasudeva’s sons starting with Kīrtman were born he afflicted us even more. When Nārada spoke to Kaṁsa, the Yadus fled in all directions, but some were imprisoned. When Vasudeva had Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma taken to Gokula, we were terrified of Kaṁsa’s harassment. Later we could not go to many places because of the Jarāsandha’s constant obstructions. We then came to Dvārakā. On understanding that you were burned in the house of lac, we began to grieve privately. But now, when your sons have obtained half of the Kuru kingdom (etarhi), by the favor of the Lord (daivena), we have obtained our homes with no disturbance to our families. O sister! This is known to you who desire to hear of our condition.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    We are viciously tormented (pra-tāpitāḥ) by Kaṁsa but now (etarhi) have returned home because of the Lord, not by our own powers. We are not independent. O sister (svasaḥ)! As a sister you have heard all of this. Or pacifying her, he gives her joy. The pilgrimage took place after killing Kaṁsa, after Draupadī’s marriage and possibly right after Yudhiṣṭḥira attained half the kingdom. It definitely took place after the rājasūya sacrifice, for Yudhiṣṭhira was present at the pilgrimage (verse 26) and Jarāsandha and Śiśupāla were absent. Though it was a long time after the harassment of Kaṁsa, Vasudeva says “now” to comfort Kuntī. Or out of fear of Kaṁsa, the Yadus who had gone away, finally gathered now, attaining a complete situation. Or only after the killing of Vidūratha, at this time, could the Yadus live peacefully.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Discriminative WisdomShanti (Peace / Tranquility)Abhaya (Fearlessness)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Aparigraha (Non-possessiveness)Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)Daksya (Resourcefulness / Skill)
    Related verses
    BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 5.12 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 7.8 BG 13.1 BG 12.6 BG 14.1
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Birds scatter from one tree when the hunter approaches and find one another again at a distant grove. Kaṁsa's tyranny scattered the Yadu-kula; Bhagavān's daiva has brought them home. The same Providence does both.

    Stories
    The Yadu-vaṁśa's exile under Kaṁsa

    Before Kṛṣṇa's appearance, Kaṁsa imprisoned Vasudeva and Devakī, killed their first six sons, and harassed the entire Yadu clan into hiding. SB 10.1-4 records the family's flight in every direction — Nanda to Vraja, the rest scattered.

    Gauḍīyas read this scattering as the necessary darkness before the Lord's appearance. The Yadus' exile under Kaṁsa makes Kṛṣṇa's līlā of liberation possible — and now, decades later at Kurukṣetra, the family that fled in fear gathers in joy. Same daiva, different season.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.22

    Important Words
    • acyuta-sandarśa — by the sight of Acyuta
      Compound: acyuta (the infallible, never-fallen) + sandarśa (full seeing). Acyuta is one of Viṣṇu's thousand names — He who never falls from His position. Darśana of Acyuta is what stabilises the kings.
    • parama-ānanda — supreme bliss
      Compound: parama (highest) + ānanda (bliss). The highest ānanda is not internal absorption but bhagavad-darśana. Gauḍīyas distinguish brahmānanda from bhakty-ānanda — the latter strictly exceeds the former (CC Madhya 6.44).
    • nirvṛtāḥ — pacified, fulfilled
      Past participle of nir-√vṛt — to come to rest, to be extinguished (in the sense of restlessness ceasing, not annihilation). The kings' world-restlessness ends at Kṛṣṇa's sight.
    • arcitāḥ — honoured
      Past participle of √arc — to worship, to honour. The Vṛṣṇis' arcana of the kings is itself sevā — receiving guests as one would receive the Deity. Gauḍīyas practise this as atithi-sevā.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Vasudeva, Ugrasena and the other Yadus honored the various kings, who became supremely blissful and content upon seeing Lord Acyuta.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Vasudeva, Ugrasena and the other Yadus honored the various kings, who became supremely blissful and content upon seeing Lord Acyuta.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Though it was suitable that they honor each other, the Yadus honored all the kings since it was proper to respect them since they were the best kings. They were completely filled with bliss which was greater than realizing Brahman because of directly seeing Kṛṣṇa, who by his nature was full of all beauty (acyutasya).

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Visitors who have travelled far are not refreshed by food and drink alone — one look at the host of their long longing settles every tiredness. The kings' fatigue from the road dissolves not in the lakes but in Acyuta's darśana.

    Important Words
    • śrī-niketam vapuḥ — the form which is the abode of opulence
      Compound: śrī (opulence, beauty, Lakṣmī) + niketam (abode) + vapuḥ (form). Kṛṣṇa's body is itself the residence of Śrī — Lakṣmī never leaves Him. Gauḍīyas note: this aiśvarya is what the kings see; the gopīs later in this chapter will see mādhurya, which exceeds it.
    • śaureḥ — of Kṛṣṇa
      Genitive of Śauri — descendant of Śūrasena, son of Vasudeva. The kṣatriya-name of Kṛṣṇa, fitting for this assembly of kings. The same Bhagavān is named differently by different rasas — Śauri here, Govinda there, Mukunda elsewhere.
    • sa-stṛīkam — along with His wives
      Compound: sa (with) + stṛīkam (with women, wives; source spelling). The Dvārakā-Kṛṣṇa appears with His sixteen-thousand-and-eight queens — full mādhurya in its aiśvarya register. Distinct from the Vraja-līlā the gopīs will reclaim below.
    • vismitāḥ — amazed
      Past participle of vi-√smi — to be astonished. Vismaya is one of the rasas; here the kings' rasa toward Kṛṣṇa is precisely vismaya — the awed wonder appropriate to aiśvarya-darśana, in contrast to the gopīs' mādhurya.
    • yudhiṣṭhiram anuvratāḥ — following Yudhiṣṭhira
      Compound phrase: yudhiṣṭhiram (accusative) + anuvratāḥ (devoted to, following). Yudhiṣṭhira, the dharma-rāja, leads the kings — but he himself bows to Kṛṣṇa. Gauḍīyas honour the pattern: even the foremost dhārmika is a servant of the Lord.
    All the royalty present, including Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī and her sons, the Pāṇḍavas and their wives, Kuntī, Sañjaya, Vidura, Kṛpācārya, Kuntībhoja, Virāṭa, Bhīṣmaka, the great Nagnajit, Purujit, Drupada, Śalya, Dhṛṣṭaketu, Kāśirāja, Damaghoṣa, Viśālākṣa, Maithila, Madra, Kekaya, Yudhāmanyu, Suśarmā, Bāhlika with his associates and their sons, and the many other kings subservient to Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira — all of them, O best of kings, were simply amazed to see the transcendental form of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the abode of all opulence and beauty, standing before them with His consorts.
    Purport

    According to Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī, all these kings were now followers of Yudhiṣṭhira because he had subjugated each of them to earn the privilege of performing the Rājasūya sacrifice. The Vedic injunctions state that a kṣatriya who wants to execute the Rājasūya for elevation to heaven must first send out a “victory horse” to roam freely; any other king whose territory this horse enters must either voluntarily submit or face the kṣatriya or his representatives in battle.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    All the royalty present, including Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī and her sons, the Pāṇḍavas and their wives, Kuntī, Saṣjaya, Vidura, Kṛpācārya, Kuntībhoja, Virāṭa, Bhīṣmaka, the great Nagnajit, Purujit, Drupada, Śalya, Dhṛṣṭaketu, Kāśirāja, Damaghoṣa, Viśālākṣa, Maithila, Madra, Kekaya, Yudhāmanyu, Suśarmā, Bāhlika with his associates and their sons, and the many other kings subservient to Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira—all of them, O best of kings, were simply amazed to see the transcendental form of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the abode of all opulence and beauty, standing before them with His consorts.
    Commentary

    At this time Yudhisthira had been given half the kingdom. Therefore he is described as having following of kings (anuvratah).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The names of the Kings are listed to show the excellence of Kṛṣṇa as the cause their experiencing great bliss. Ambikā putra indicates Dhṛtarāṣṭra’s blindness (since Ambikā closed her eyes on seeing Vyāsa). Gandharī’s sons included Duryodhana. Tathā means “all.” The wives of the Pāṇḍavas included Draupadī. Nagnajit is called great (mahān) because he gave his daughter to Kṛṣṇa. Thus he was considered the best among the kings. Purujit or Śaibya was the father of Mitrāvindā. The king of Kāśī was the brother of Sudakṣiṇa, or another person who lived with him out of great affection. Viśālākṣa-maithila means Bahulāśva or another king of that province. The word ādayaḥ indicates all their followers. Jarāsandha and Śiśupāla are not mentioned because they did not come out of hatred, understanding that Kṛṣṇa was there. Those who were dedicated to Yudhiṣṭhira, along with their wives, were amazed at directly seeing (vi—īkṣya) Kṛṣṇa, the abode of all beauty (śrīniketam). This means that at this time Yudhiṣṭhira had obtained half the kingdom. The wives were especially amazed because they were naturally soft with prema. Or the women and the men equally were amazed.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Nyaya (Justice / Righteous Judgment)Daya (Compassion / Mercy)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Kritajnata (Gratitude)Discriminative WisdomAtma Nigraha (Self-control)Satya (Truthfulness)
    Related verses
    BG 8.4 BG 12.1 SB 1.2BG 12.3 BG 12.2 BG 7.1 BG 4.1 SB 4.29.1 BG 16.2 BG 18.65
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A great assembly hall is built of many pillars — kings, ṛṣis, foster-parents, friends — but every pillar inclines toward the central figure. The Mahābhārata's whole cast gathers here, and every eye is on one Person.

    Stories
    Bhīṣma's recognition of Kṛṣṇa as svayaṁ-bhagavān

    Of all who saw Kṛṣṇa at Kurukṣetra, Bhīṣma had seen Him most fully — as friend, as charioteer, as the Person who refused to fight yet ruled the battle. In SB 1.9, on his bed of arrows, Bhīṣma identifies Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Person whose three-bending form alone he wished to see.

    Gauḍīyas regard Bhīṣma as one of the twelve mahājanas (SB 6.3.20). His presence in this verse — sitting amazed beside Droṇa and Dhṛtarāṣṭra — is the assembly's silent vote: even those who hesitated through the Mahābhārata know now whom they are seeing.

    📝 My note

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.27

    Important Words
    • rāma-kṛṣṇābhyām — by Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa
      Dual instrumental. The two yoked brothers honour the kings together. Gauḍīya tradition pairs Them as kṛṣṇa-balarāmau — Balarāma's worship is the doorway to Kṛṣṇa-bhakti (cf. CC Ādi 5).
    • kṛṣṇa-parigrahān — the personal associates of Kṛṣṇa
      Compound: kṛṣṇa + parigrahān (accepted ones, associates). Parigraha — those whom He has personally accepted. In Gauḍīya theology, the Lord's parikara (eternal associates) are honoured equally with Him — bhaktya-yutānāṁ bhaktānām (BG 10.10).
    • praśaśaṁsuḥ — they praised
      Perfect of pra-√śaṁs — to praise highly. Praśaṁsā of the Lord's devotees is itself a limb of bhakti (cf. SB 11.2.46 — viṣṇu-bhakta-jana-priyaḥ, the bhāgavata loves the Lord's people).
    • samarhaṇāḥ — appropriate tokens of honour
      Compound: sam (proper) + arhaṇa (worship, honour). The right form of honour — fitting the recipient. Gauḍīya etiquette is exact: each devotee is honoured according to his bhāva-stage, not flattened into generic respect.
    After Lord Balarāma and Lord Kṛṣṇa had liberally honored them, with great joy and enthusiasm these kings began to praise the members of the Vṛṣṇi clan, Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s personal associates.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    After Lord Balarāma and Lord Kṛṣṇa had liberally honored them, with great joy and enthusiasm these kings began to praise the members of the Vṛṣṇi clan, Śrī Kṛṣṇa's personal associates.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After (atha) Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma personally honored them, the kings praised the Vṛṣṇis who had wealth or associates. Parigraha can mean wealth, associates, accepting and root.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After that (atha), they were honored by Kṛṣṇa, who gave them bliss, and Balarāma who gave them enjoyment. Previously they had been honored by the best of the Yādavas like Vasudeva. Now the chief kings were honored by the two Lords since they were the most important persons there. The kings were worshipped with the best item (sam-arhanāḥ) in the best way (samyak) by the supremely excellent Lords. Then completely (ā yuktāḥ) joyful (mudāḥ), they praised the Yadus because they were the servants (parigrahān) of svayam bhagavān, who appeared to display his unlimited powers (kṛṣṇa), because they had the root of all wealth and family in the form of Kṛṣna (kṛṣṇa-parigrahān).

    Related values & verses
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Related lectures
    • Romapada Sw SB 10-82-27-30 - 2015-12-15 ISKCON Alachua
      His Holiness Romapada Swami· 2015-12-15· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    When the king is exalted, his courtiers shine; when Kṛṣṇa is honoured, the Yādavas glow in His reflected light. The kings' praise of the Vṛṣṇis is itself indirect Kṛṣṇa-stuti.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.28

    అహో భోజపతే యూయం జన్మభాజో నృణామిహ । యత్ పశ్యథాసకృత్ కృష్ణం దుర్దర్శమపి యోగినామ్ ॥ ౨౮ ॥

    Important Words
    • janma-bhājaḥ — having taken a worthwhile birth
      Compound: janma (birth) + bhājaḥ (partaker of). A 'birth that shares in something worthwhile' — Gauḍīyas read this as proximity to bhagavad-bhaktas or Bhagavān Himself. Mere human birth is not enough.
    • durdarśam — rarely seen
      Compound: dur (difficult) + darśam (seeing). Kṛṣṇa is durdarśa even for yogīs — they see Paramātmā within, but not the full Person. The Vṛṣṇis see Him in His Bhagavān-form. Bhāgavata's three-tier realisation (Brahman, Paramātmā, Bhagavān) is operative here.
    • asakṛt — repeatedly
      Compound: a (not) + sakṛt (once). Not once but again and again. The everyday continuity of darśana — Vraja's gift to its residents, Dvārakā's gift to the Bhojas. Gauḍīyas long for this same nitya-darśana.
    • bhoja-pate — O master of the Bhojas
      Vocative compound: bhoja (the clan) + pate (lord). Ugrasena addressed. His clan name 'Bhoja' shares its root with bhoga — but the verse subtly redirects: real bhoga is having Kṛṣṇa, not enjoyment of the world.
    [The kings said:] O King of the Bhojas, you alone among men have achieved a truly exalted birth, for you continually behold Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is rarely visible even to great yogīs.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    [The kings said:] O King of the Bhojas, you alone among men have achieved a truly exalted birth, for you continually behold Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is rarely visible even to great yogīs.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The kings address Ugrasena since he was recognized as the leader, being the king of the Yadus, or because they treated him with respect as a king, equal to themselves. Aho indicates respect or amazement. It is rare that yogīs like the Kumāras can see Kṛṣṇa directly even once, and after great difficulties.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Tapas (Austerity / Discipline)Atma Nigraha (Self-control)Shaucha (Cleanliness / Purity)Dhriti (Resilience / Steadfastness / Fortitude)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Shraddha (Faith / Reverence)Dhairya (Composure / Courage)Ahimsa (Non-violence / Compassion)
    Related verses
    BG 17.14 BG 17.15 BG 6.35 BG 2.66 BG 17.16 BG 13.8
    Concepts
    JapaJivaYoga
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Yogis climb the mountain for decades hoping for one glimpse of the summit's flag; the king of Bhojas walks his own house and sees Bhagavān every morning. Birth in His circle is more valuable than any yogic attainment.

    Stories
    Ugrasena restored to his throne

    Ugrasena, Kaṁsa's father, had been deposed and imprisoned by his own son. When Kṛṣṇa killed Kaṁsa (SB 10.44), He immediately restored Ugrasena to the throne of Mathurā and offered Him daily respect as His elder.

    Gauḍīyas read this verse as the kings' recognition that Ugrasena's birth, on which he himself never reflected, was extraordinarily blessed — he sees Kṛṣṇa repeatedly, durdarśam api yoginām. The yogīs' object of distant meditation is Ugrasena's son-in-law's nephew, sitting next to him at dinner.

    📝 My note

    Important Words
    • pāda-avanejana-payaḥ — the water from washing His feet
      Compound: pāda (foot) + avanejana (washing) + payaḥ (water). Caraṇāmṛta — the water that has touched Kṛṣṇa's feet purifies the universe. Gauḍīyas honour caraṇāmṛta as one of the five great offerings (pañcāmṛta-snāna's heart).
    • viśrutiḥ — fame
      From vi-√śru — to be widely heard. Kṛṣṇa's śruti-glorification is itself purifying. Gauḍīyas: hearing the name purifies; this is the doctrine of nāma-prabhāva.
    • niraya-vartmani — upon the path of hell
      Compound: niraya (hell) + vartmani (on the path, locative). The verse names ordinary gṛhastha-life as niraya-vartman — yet declares the Yādavas safe on it because Viṣṇu walks beside them. Gauḍīyas: family life without Kṛṣṇa is the hell-path; with Him, it is Vaikuṇṭha-vartman.
    • svarga-apavarga-viramaḥ — the cessation of (desire for) heaven and liberation
      Compound: svarga (heaven) + apavarga (liberation) + viramaḥ (cessation). Kṛṣṇa's presence ends interest in both svarga and mokṣa — the Gauḍīya signature. Bhakti exceeds liberation; nothing else is needed once He is seen.
    • sa-yauna sa-piṇḍa bandhaḥ — relationships through marriage and blood
      Compound phrase. The Yādavas' relationship to Kṛṣṇa is sa-yauna (marital) and sa-piṇḍa (ancestral). Gauḍīyas note: these are vraja-style sambandhas (sakhya, vātsalya, mādhurya) raised to ontological status — bhakti through the natural ties of life.
    • viṣṇuḥ — the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu in person
      Compound phrase. Svayam — Himself, not by deputy. Echoes SB 1.3.28's kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam. The kings are correctly identifying the Person at Kurukṣetra.
    His fame, as broadcast by the Vedas, the water that has washed His feet, and the words He speaks in the form of the revealed scriptures — these thoroughly purify this universe. Although the earth’s good fortune was ravaged by time, the touch of His lotus feet has revitalized her, and thus she is raining down on us the fulfillment of all our desires. The same Lord Viṣṇu who makes one forget the goals of heaven and liberation has now entered into marital and blood relationships with you, who otherwise travel on the hellish path of family life. Indeed, in these relationships you see and touch Him directly, walk beside Him, converse with Him, and together with Him lie down to rest, sit at ease and take your meals.
    Purport

    All Vedic mantras glorify Lord Viṣṇu; this truth is supported with elaborate evidence by learned ācāryas like Rāmānuja, in his Vedārtha-saṅgraha, and Madhva, in his Ṛg-veda-bhāṣya. The words Viṣṇu Himself speaks, such as the Bhagavad-gītā, are the confidential essence of all scripture. In His manifestation as Vyāsadeva, the Supreme Lord composed both the Vedānta-sutras and Mahābhārata, and this Mahābhārata includes Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s personal statement: vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo/ vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham. “By all the Vedas, I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of Vedānta, and I am the knower of the Vedas. ” ( Bhagavad-gītā 15.15 ) When Lord Viṣṇu appeared before Bali Mahārāja to beg three steps of land, the Lord’s second step pierced the shells of the universe. The water of the transcendental river Virajā, lying just outside the universal egg, thus seeped inside, washing Lord Vāmana’s foot and flowing down to become the Ganges River. Because of the sanctity of its origin, the Ganges is generally considered the most holy of rivers. But even more potent is the water of the Yamunā, where Lord Viṣṇu in His original form of Govinda played with His intimate companions. In these two verses the assembled kings praise the special merit of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Yadu clan. Not only do they see Kṛṣṇa, but they are also directly connected with Him by dual bonds of marital and blood relationships. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī suggests that the word bandha, beside its more obvious meaning of “relation,” can also be understood in the sense of “capture,” expressing that the love the Yadus feel for the Lord obliges Him always to stay with them.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    His fame, as broadcast by the Vedas, the water that has washed His feet, and the words He speaks in the form of the revealed scriptures—these thoroughly purify this universe. Although the earth's good fortune was ravaged by time, the touch of His lotus feet has revitalized her, and thus she is raining down on us the fulfillment of all our desires. The same Lord Viṣṇu who makes one forget the goals of heaven and liberation has now entered into marital and blood relationships with you, who otherwise travel on the hellish path of family life. Indeed, in these relationships you see and touch Him directly, walk beside Him, converse with Him, and together with Him lie down to rest, sit at ease and take your meals.
    Commentary

    Krsna, whose fame (visrutih) is praised by the Vedas, thoroughly purifies this universe (idam alam). He whose foot water, the Ganga also purifies the universe; whose words in the form of scriptures called the Vedas purify the universe, the touch of whose feet revives the energy of the earth, whose glory is burned by time, and which rains upon us all the material needs, is present here (verb is in next verse) . Krsna, with whom you have relation through marriage and descent, which brings about personally seeing him, touching him, walking with him talking with him, sleeping, sitting and eating with him, and who caused distaste for heaven and liberation for the sinless persons (niraya vartma nivartanam), has appeared in your house.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    His fame, his foot water and his words (successively more powerful) purify the universe. Having stated ancient, standard glories of the Lord, they give particulars. The earth’s good fortune was ravaged by Kali, which had entered at that time. Since his feet are soft like the lotus, he should not touch the earth with his feet. But by the touch of his feet the earth has become most fortunate and developed power of giving all things. It now showers us with all things continuously. He is related to you through marriage or he is your relative Otherwise you would going to hell. This is a statement made from the commoner’s viewpoint. (The Vṛṣṇis never go to hell.) He has made you give up the taste for the happiness of Brahman realization and material enjoyment of Svarga by giving special happiness in seeing him without your having to endeavor. Or he is related to you who are on the path of escape from saṁsāra (nirayam). He is directly pervading everything as Bhagavān (viṣṇuḥ).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    His fame, his foot water and his words (successively more powerful) purify the universe. Having stated ancient, standard glories of the Lord, they give particulars. The earth’s good fortune was ravaged by Kali, which had entered at that time. Since Kṛṣṇa’s feet are soft like the lotus, he should not touch the earth with his feet. But by the touch of his feet the earth has become most fortunate and developed the power of giving all things. It now showers us with all things continuously. He is related to you through marriage or he is your relative. Otherwise you would go to hell. This is a statement made from the commoner’s viewpoint. (The Vṛṣṇis never go to hell.) He has made you give up the taste for the happiness of Brahman realization and material enjoyment of Svarga by giving special happiness in seeing him without your having to endeavor (svayam) or directly. He is directly pervading everything as Bhagavān (viṣṇuḥ) by spreading his powers. Or though he is lord of the lords of the universe, he remains as your relative (āsa) in your house (gṛhe). In each of his houses, he causes detachment from Svarga and liberation by seeing him. He separates these things from the devotees by showing them to be insignificant.

    • Bhanu Sw SB 10-82-29-30 - Special Mercy of Lord is descending as a family member and satisfying every devotee - ISKCON Chennai
      His Holiness Bhanu Swami· English
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A village famine ends not when the rain falls in distant mountains but when the river touches the parched field. Kṛṣṇa is not the absent monsoon of the Upaniṣads but the actual river — visible, conversing, eating beside you. The kings declare the singular gift of Kṛṣṇa's personal presence.

    Stories
    Ambarīṣa's gṛhastha bhakti

    King Ambarīṣa (SB 9.4) ruled his kingdom, raised his family, and observed Ekādaśī — yet Bhāgavata calls his life perfect bhakti. He engaged each sense in Kṛṣṇa: eyes in seeing the Deity, ears in hearing kīrtana, hands in temple sevā.

    Gauḍīyas pair Ambarīṣa with this verse's teaching: the kings declare that the Yādavas, though engaged in marriage and family (sa-yauna, sa-piṇḍa), travel a path the niraya-vartman (hellish path of household life) does not touch — because Viṣṇu Himself sits at their dinner. Family life with Him is not bondage; without Him, no renunciation saves.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.31

    ↗
    శ్రీశుక ఉవాచ నన్దస్తత్ర యదూన్ ప్రాప్తాన్ జ్ఞాత్వా కృష్ణపురోగమాన్ । తత్రాగమద్ వృతో గోపైరన:స్థార్థైర్దిద‍ృక్షయా ॥ ౩౧ ॥

    Important Words
    • nandaḥ — Nanda Mahārāja
      Kṛṣṇa's Vraja father, head of the cowherd community. Gauḍīyas hold Nanda as the personification of vātsalya-rasa together with Yaśodā — the Lord's foster parents, whose love He chose to receive.
    • didṛkṣayā — with the desire to see
      Desiderative noun from √dṛś — the eagerness for darśana. Vraja's defining bhāva: the cowherds do not seek liberation; they want to see Him. Gauḍīyas align with this — darśana, not jñāna, is the prayojana.
    • kṛṣṇa-puraḥ-gamān — with Kṛṣṇa keeping in front
      Compound: kṛṣṇa + puraḥ (in front) + gamān (going). The Yādavas with Kṛṣṇa leading — the very arrangement Nanda recognises and runs toward. Where Kṛṣṇa leads, the cowherds follow.
    • anaḥ-stha-arthaiḥ — with their possessions on wagons
      Compound: anaḥ (wagon, cart) + stha (placed) + arthaiḥ (possessions, instrumental). Nanda brings everything from Vraja — gifts of butter, curds, presents. Vraja-style bhakti always arrives bringing — never empty-handed, never ascetic-arrogant.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: When Nanda Mahārāja learned that the Yadus had arrived, led by Kṛṣṇa, he immediately went to see them. The cowherds accompanied him, their various possessions loaded on their wagons.
    Purport

    The cowherds of Vraja were planning to stay at Kurukṣetra for some days, so they came equipped with adequate provisions, especially milk products and other foods for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: When Nanda Mahārāja learned that the Yadus had arrived, led by Kṛṣṇa, he immediately went to see them. The cowherds accompanied him, their various possessions loaded on their wagons.
    Commentary

    Nanda was surrounded with cowherd men on carts loaded with goods. These supplies had been brought there in order to feed Krsna and live together with Krsna.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After describing the conversations between Kuntī and Vasudeva and others briefly in the beginning, there is now a detailed description of the Lord on his arrival with Nanda and others who had arrived previous to the eclipse, since that was their main goal in going there, but had difficulty meeting him because of many reasons stated previously. The description continues till the end of the chapter. Understanding that the Yadus had arrived, along with all their families (pra-āptān), headed by Kṛṣṇa, his son, who attracts everyone’s heart, Nanda came to Kurukṣetra, surrounded by Upananda and other cowherds, with their wives and others, in order to see him. Forget about others going for worldly or otherworldly benefits. They came there just with a desire to see Kṛṣṇa. This indicates their condition in separation from Kṛṣṇa. They came with carts loaded with goods or with articles dear to Kṛṣṇa, with his beloved gopīs sitting in carts.

    Analogy

    A father long separated from his son does not wait to be summoned — the moment he hears the boy has come, he loads the wagon and goes. Nanda's response is instant; vātsalya does not negotiate.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.32

    Important Words
    • tanvaḥ prāṇam iva utthitāḥ — rising as if bodies receiving their vital air
      Phrase: tanvaḥ (bodies) + prāṇam (life-air) + iva (as if) + utthitāḥ (risen). The Bhāgavata's bold image — separation from a beloved devotee is death; reunion is resurrection. Gauḍīyas read vaiṣṇava-saṅga in exactly this register.
    • cira-darśana-kātarāḥ — agitated from (not) seeing for a long time
      Compound: cira (long time) + darśana (seeing) + kātarāḥ (agitated, distressed). The technical vocabulary of viraha — long absence of darśana causes kātaratā. Rūpa Gosvāmī catalogues this state in detail.
    • pariṣaśvajire gāḍham — they embraced firmly
      Phrase: pariṣaśvajire (perfect of pari-√svaj, embraced around) + gāḍham (tightly). Embrace as bhakti's bodily idiom. Note this verse already foreshadows verse 39's interior embrace — the gopīs will take Him into their hearts.
    • hṛṣṭāḥ — delighted
      Past participle of √hṛṣ — to thrill. Hṛṣṭa is the standard Bhāgavata term for the delight of bhakti-meeting. The Lord's name Hṛṣīkeśa (master of the senses) shares this root — He thrills the senses of His devotees.
    Seeing Nanda, the Vṛṣṇis were delighted and stood up like dead bodies coming back to life. Having felt much distress at not seeing him for so long, they held him in a tight embrace.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Seeing Nanda, the Vṛṣṇis were delighted and stood up like dead bodies coming back to life. Having felt much distress at not seeing him for so long, they held him in a tight embrace.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Seated in an assembly when Kṛṣṇa was not present, the Vṛṣṇis, joyful, stood up as if their hands, feet and other limbs had regained their chief life air after fainting. In immediately standing up at the same time, they showed that they were all dependent for life upon Nanda. This act also showed Nanda’s great qualities. They were overcome with seeing him after a long time. Or they were overjoyed in seeing him after a long time. Thus they embraced him intensely.

    Analogy

    A body without prāṇa is no body — when prāṇa returns, the limbs rise. The Vṛṣṇis without Nanda were corpses of grief; his arrival is the prāṇa that lifts them.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.33

    వసుదేవ: పరిష్వజ్య సమ్ప్రీత: ప్రేమవిహ్వల: । స్మరన్ కంసకృతాన్ క్లేశాన్ పుత్రన్యాసం చ గోకులే ॥ ౩౩ ॥

    Important Words
    • prema-vihvalaḥ — beside himself due to love
      Compound: prema (perfected love) + vihvalaḥ (overwhelmed, helpless). The exact Gauḍīya phrase for prema's bodily impact. Vihvalatā — the soul shaken open by love it cannot contain. Mahāprabhu's biographies are saturated with this word.
    • putra-nyāsam — the leaving of his sons (in trust)
      Compound: putra (son) + nyāsam (deposit, entrusting). Nyāsa is a legal-religious term — a sacred trust. Vasudeva deposited his sons with Nanda; the trust held. Gauḍīyas read all bhakti as ātma-nikṣepa — the soul deposited with the Lord.
    • kaṁsa-kṛtān kleśān — the troubles created by Kaṁsa
      Compound phrase: kaṁsa (the tyrant) + kṛtān (made) + kleśān (afflictions, accusative plural). Kleśas are the standard Vaiṣṇava category for affliction (cf. avidyā-asmitā-rāga-dveṣa-abhiniveśa, but here in social-historical form).
    • samprītaḥ — overjoyed
      Compound: sam (fully) + prītaḥ (loved, satisfied). Prīti — affectionate satisfaction. The relief of grief through bhakti-meeting; not absence of feeling but feeling's right fulfilment.
    Vasudeva embraced Nanda Mahārāja with great joy. Beside himself with ecstatic love, Vasudeva remembered the troubles Kaṁsa had caused him, forcing him to leave his sons in Gokula for Their safety.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Vasudeva embraced Nanda Mahārāja with great joy. Beside himself with ecstatic love, Vasudeva remembered the troubles Kaṁsa had caused him, forcing him to leave his sons in Gokula for Their safety.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Having described the Vṛṣṇis’ reaction in general, now Vasudeva’s detailed reaction is described. More than the others, Vasudeva was completely overjoyed and then overcome with prema—with ecstatic symptoms like goose bumps and trembling. He remembered how Kaṁsa had imprisoned him and killed his other sons and how he had put his son or his two sons in Gokula. He remembered the previous incidents on seeing Nanda, because of recalling the great help Nanda had been in freeing him from suffering.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Discriminative WisdomAbhaya (Fearlessness)Seva (Service / Selfless Action)Samata (Equanimity / Even-mindedness)Anahankarah (Freedom from False Ego)
    Related verses
    BG 2.60 BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 6.1 BG 9.26 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 18.1 BG 3.1 BG 7.8 BG 3.19
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Some embraces hold not just the body in your arms but the years they survived through. Vasudeva clasps Nanda and inside the embrace is the night he carried the infant Kṛṣṇa across the Yamunā — every grief that gift cost him.

    Stories
    Vasudeva carrying Kṛṣṇa to Gokula

    On the night of Kṛṣṇa's birth, with Kaṁsa hunting newborns, Vasudeva carried his eighth child through chains that fell open, doors that unbarred themselves, and a Yamunā that parted (SB 10.3-4). He laid Kṛṣṇa beside the sleeping Yaśodā and took her newborn daughter back to the prison.

    For decades Vasudeva did not see the son he had carried. Embracing Nanda at Kurukṣetra, all of that returns — putra-nyāsam ca gokule, the leaving of his sons in Gokula. Gauḍīyas treasure this verse as proof that Vraja vātsalya is not just Nanda-Yaśodā's privilege; Vasudeva's heart contains the same fire.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.34

    ↗
    కృష్ణరామౌ పరిష్వజ్య పితరావభివాద్య చ । న కిఞ్చనోచతు: ప్రేమ్ణా సాశ్రుకణ్ఠౌ కురూద్వహ ॥ ౩౪ ॥
    kṛṣṇa-rāmau pariṣvajya
    [krishna-raamau parishvajya]
    pitarāv abhivādya ca
    [pitaraav abhivaadya cha]

    [na kinchanochatuh premnaa]

    [saashru-kanthau kuroodvaha]

    kṛṣṇa - rāmau — Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma pariṣvajya — embracing pitarau — Their parents abhivādya — offering respects ca — and na kiñcana — nothing ūcatuḥ — said premṇā — with love sa - aśru — full of tears kaṇṭhau — whose throats kuru - udvaha — O most heroic of the Kurus .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)

    O most heroic of the Kurus .
    Important Words
    • kṛṣṇa-rāmau — Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma
      Dual nominative. The two brothers acting together. Gauḍīyas note: Their joint bow to Nanda-Yaśodā models filial-vātsalya — even Bhagavān honours His foster parents. Aiśvarya melts before vātsalya here.
    • premṇā — with love
      Instrumental of preman — perfected love (prema). The cause of the choked throats: prema, not grief. Gauḍīyas distinguish prema's tears (rapturous) from śoka's tears (mournful) — both run, but the source differs.
    • sa-aśru-kaṇṭhau — whose throats were full of tears
      Compound: sa (with) + aśru (tears) + kaṇṭhau (throats, dual). Gadgada — the choked voice — is one of Rūpa Gosvāmī's eight sāttvika-bhāvas. Even Kṛṣṇa Himself displays it; Bhagavān is not above the bodily signs of love.
    • na kiñcana ūcatuḥ — they said nothing
      Phrase: na (not) + kiñcana (anything) + ūcatuḥ (they said, dual perfect of √vac). The verbose Kṛṣṇa of the Gītā is silent before Nanda. Gauḍīyas read this as bhakti's highest register — where speech ends, prema speaks.
    O hero of the Kurus, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma embraced Their foster parents and bowed down to them, but Their throats were so choked up with tears of love that the two Lords could say nothing.
    Purport

    After a long separation, a respectful child should first offer obeisances to his parents. Nanda and Yaśodā gave their sons no opportunity for this, however, for as soon as they saw Them they embraced Them. Only then could Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma offer Their proper respects.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    O hero of the Kurus, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma embraced Their foster parents and bowed down to them, but Their throats were so choked up with tears of love that the two Lords could say nothing.
    Commentary

    Without delay Nanda and Yasoda embraced Krsna and Balarama, and because of that, they embraced their parents as described in this verse. Then they addressed their parents (abhivadya). But they could not say anything, because their throats were choked with tears.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma were with Vasudeva. Upananda and other cowherds were also there. Yaśodā’s heart had almost stopped functioning and on the way, she had almost not eaten, her face became filled with tears. She had to be held by the arms by friends on either side. Hearing that she had come in a cloth covered cart and was not far away, immediately Vasudeva along with intimate associates took her and Nanda inside his residence. This verse describes what happened when they met Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma who had been called. Though Nanda and Yaśodā, coming from Vraja, met with Vasudeva and others who were seated in an outer room, and would have gone into an inner room where Devakī and other women were seated, which was proper behavior in front of many men, the descriptions of their meeting the men and women are made at once in order to show that both groups had equal prema for Nanda and Yaśodā and to make the description brief. One should understand that Devakī and Yaśodā also embraced and talked together. Kṛṣṇa who was attracted (kṛṣ means being attracted) to the great affection of his parents and Rāma who took pleasure (ṛāma means pleased) in the affection of his parents, simultaneously embraced their two parents. Recovering after a long time, they then addressed them. On seeing their parents they approached them, embraced them, and could not free themselves from their embrace, nor could their parents free themselves from the embrace. Somehow they managed to sit down but could not speak--Kṛṣṇa and Balarāṁa could not inquire about their health since tears blocked their throats, which happened because of prema. They were filled with affection for their parents and their parents were filled with affection for them. This should be understood in all the descriptions. It should also be understood that they trembled and their hairs stood on end. Kṛṣṇa is mentioned first because he desired to go first, out of his great attraction to their affection. Or out of great affection for him, Balarāma placed Kṛṣṇa in front of him. Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma always regarded them as their parents (pitarau). Out of intense prema Śukadeva calls to Parīkṣit “O best of the Kurus!” Such nature and conduct is suitable for those coming in a great family of which you are a similar member.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

    Related verses
    BG 2.60 BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 6.1 BG 9.26 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 18.1 BG 3.1 BG 7.8
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    There are reunions for which speech is too small a vessel. Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, gods of all eloquence, fall silent before Nanda and Yaśodā — tears speak what mantras cannot.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.35

    ↗
    తావాత్మాసనమారోప్య బాహుభ్యాం పరిరభ్య చ । యశోదా చ మహాభాగా సుతౌ విజహతు: శుచ: ॥ ౩౫ ॥

    [taav aatmaasanam aaropya]
    bāhubhyāṁ parirabhya ca
    [baahubhyaam parirabhya cha]

    [yashodaa cha mahaa-bhaagaa]

    [sutau vijahatuh shuchah]
    Word-for-word
    tau — the two of Them ātma - āsanam — onto their laps āropya — raising bāhubhyām — with their arms parirabhya — embracing ca — and yaśodā — mother Yaśodā ca — also mahā - bhāgā — saintly sutau — their sons vijahatuḥ — they gave up śucaḥ — their sorrow .

    Important Words
    • yaśodā — mother Yaśodā
      The personification of vātsalya-rasa. Her name means 'giver of fame' — and she gave Kṛṣṇa the fame of being a son. Gauḍīyas hold her as the supreme vātsalya-bhakta; Brahmā at SB 10.14.32 envies her fortune.
    • ātma-āsanam — onto their laps (their own seat)
      Compound: ātma (own) + āsanam (seat). The lap is the parent's own throne — and they put Bhagavān there. Aiśvarya inverted: He who sits on Vaikuṇṭha's throne sits on Yaśodā's lap.
    • mahā-bhāgā — saintly, greatly fortunate
      Compound: mahā (great) + bhāgā (share, fortune). The same epithet given to the Yādavas in verse 9 — but here singular feminine for Yaśodā alone. She is the most bhāgyavatī of all jīvas: the Supreme is her child.
    • vijahatuḥ śucaḥ — they gave up their sorrow (dual)
      Phrase: vijahatuḥ (perfect dual of vi-√hā, gave up) + śucaḥ (sorrow). Mirroring verse 17 (Kuntī's jahau śucaḥ) — the chapter has now lifted three great griefs (Kuntī's, the Vṛṣṇis', Yaśodā's). Same mechanism: bhagavad-darśana plus bhakta-saṅga.
    Raising their two sons onto their laps and holding Them in their arms, Nanda and saintly mother Yaśodā forgot their sorrow.
    Purport

    Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains that after the initial embraces and obeisances, Vasudeva led Nanda and Yaśodā into his tent as they held the hands of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. Following them inside were Rohiṇī, other women and men of Vraja, and a number of attendants. Inside, Nanda and Yaśodā took the two boys on their laps. Despite having heard the glories of the two Lords of Dvārakā, and despite seeing these opulences now before their eyes, Nanda and Yaśodā looked upon Them as if They were still their eight-year-old children.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Raising their two sons onto their laps and holding Them in their arms, Nanda and saintly mother Yaśodā forgot their sorrow.
    Commentary

    Then Vasudeva made Nanda and Yasoda along with their companions, holding the hands of Krsna and Balarama enter a tent. Then what did they do? This verse answers. First Nanda and then Yasoda with an eager heart welcomed their sons. How was it possible for them to offer their own seats to their sons who now showed such opulences? Out of spontaneous parental love, they thought of Krsna and Balaramas as being eight years old offspring eternally (sutau), though they had heard about and seen their opulences and powers. When Krsna embraced by Nanda and Yasoda sat on their laps, he transformed into a cowherd boy completely. It was in this form he had while sitting that he also embraced the gopis. Thus it is described that they gave up their lamentation in separation (sucah).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Vasudeva, according to the rules for welcoming guests, and seeing that everyone might fall down because of being overcome with affection at that time, made them all sit. Then their parents immediately put the two boys on the seats in which the parents sat. Śukadeva indicates that the name Yaśodā was suitable since she gave (dā) such good fortune (yaśo) to Śukadeva because he could describe her prema at this time. She herself was most fortunate (mahā-bhāgāḥ). Ca indicates Nanda. He also, most fortunate, was there. She however is specifically named to indicate her extreme affection. “How could the two parents place Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma on their seats without fear since the two showed such evident powers and were fully grown up?” They always regarded Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma with affection as their sons (sutau). They could not give their sons up at all.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

    BG 2.60 BG 5.7 BG 12.5 BG 5.4 BG 6.1 BG 9.26 BG 3.27 BG 10.4 BG 3.11 BG 18.1 BG 3.1 BG 2.55 BG 7.8
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    A nest emptied by years can hold its birds again in one moment — Nanda and Yaśodā lift Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma onto their laps and the long ache vanishes. The lap of vātsalya is the only place that fits Bhagavān as a son.

    Stories
    Yaśodā with the rope (SB 10.9)

    Once Yaśodā tried to bind little Kṛṣṇa to a mortar with a rope, and every rope she added fell two fingers short. Bhāgavata calls this dāmodara-līlā — the Lord who consents to be bound by the rope of His mother's love alone.

    Here at Kurukṣetra, that same Yaśodā holds Him on her lap and forgets every sorrow. Gauḍīyas read the two scenes as one continuous vātsalya-stream: the rope of love that fell short in Vraja still binds Him at Kurukṣetra. He came because she called.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.36

    Important Words
    • vraja-īśvarīm — the Queen of Vraja (Yaśodā)
      Compound: vraja (the cowherd country) + īśvarīm (queen, feminine). Yaśodā as īśvarī — sovereign of Vraja. Gauḍīyas honour this title: Vraja is her kingdom, and Kṛṣṇa is her subject in vātsalya-rasa.
    • maitṛīm — friendship
      Accusative form as printed in the source. Maitrī — friendship rooted in maitra (well-wishing, from mitra). Not mere acquaintance but principled solidarity. Gauḍīya bhakti recognises this between Vraja and Mathurā mothers: shared sevā of one Lord.
    • bāṣpa-kaṇṭhyau — in whose throats were tears
      Compound: bāṣpa (tears) + kaṇṭhyau (throats, dual feminine). Repetition of verse 34's gadgada-bhāva — the chapter's recurring sāttvika sign. Vaiṣṇava reunions are wet-eyed by definition.
    • samūcatuḥ — they addressed (her)
      Perfect dual of sam-√vac. Speech finally arrives — after the embrace, after the tears, the words form. Gauḍīyas read this order as exact: prema first, language after.
    Then Rohiṇī and Devakī both embraced the Queen of Vraja, remembering the faithful friendship she had shown them. Their throats choking with tears, they addressed her as follows.
    Purport

    At this time, according to Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Śrī Vasudeva invited Nanda outside to meet Ugrasena and the other elder Yadus. Taking this opportunity, Rohiṇī and Devakī talked with Queen Yaśodā.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Then Rohiṇī and Devakī both embraced the Queen of Vraja, remembering the faithful friendship she had shown them. Their throats choking with tears, they addressed her as follows.
    Commentary

    Then, Nanda, being called by Vasudeva, went outside to meet Ugrasena and others, while Rohini and Devaki met with Yasoda. When they entered, they ( and Subhdra) embraced her as described in this verse.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    After Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma had come and their parents had recovered somewhat, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma went outside with Nanda and Vasudeva to meet with thousands of other residents of Vraja. Then Rohiṇī and Devakī embraced Yaśodā. Rohiṇī is mentioned before Devakī because Devakī was delayed when Kṛṣṇa lost consciousness from bliss at the meeting and because Rohiṇī had special affection for Yaśodā from living with Yaśodā in Vraja for a long time. Śukadeva calls Yaśodā the queen of Vraja out of his great bhāva, showing greater respect for her than the other two women there. Maitrīm means that she performed actions for their benefit.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    When two mothers meet who have raised the same child between them, words queue up behind tears and only the simplest praise can get through. Rohiṇī and Devakī acknowledge what Vraja did for what Mathurā could not.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.37

    ↗
    కా విస్మరేత వాం మైత్రీమనివృత్తాం వ్రజేశ్వరి । అవాప్యాప్యైన్ద్రమైశ్వర్యం యస్యా నేహ ప్రతిక్రియా ॥ ౩౭ ॥

    Important Words
    • vraja-īśvari — O Queen of Vraja
      Vocative. Direct address to Yaśodā as Vraja's sovereign. Repeated from verse 36 — emphasis. Gauḍīyas hold this title as her permanent honorific.
    • aindram aiśvaryam — the opulence of Indra
      Compound: aindram (belonging to Indra) + aiśvaryam (lordliness, wealth). Indra's wealth is the proverbial maximum of heavenly opulence — and it is named here only to be declared insufficient. Vraja-sevā exceeds Vaikuṇṭha's economy.
    • anivṛttām — unceasing friendship
      Compound phrase: anivṛttām (not turned back, continuous) + maitrīm (friendship). Vraja's love does not pulse on and off; it is continuous. Gauḍīyas honour this constancy as bhakti's true mark.
    • prati-kriyā — repayment, reciprocation
      Compound: prati (back, in return) + kriyā (act). Devakī declares no prati-kriyā is possible. Gauḍīya soteriology: the Lord's mercy and His devotees' sevā can never be repaid — only received and passed forward.
    [Rohiṇī and Devakī said:] What woman could forget the unceasing friendship you and Nanda have shown us, dear Queen of Vraja? There is no way to repay you in this world, even with the wealth of Indra.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    [Rohiṇī and Devakī said:] What woman could forget the unceasing friendship you and Nanda have shown us, dear Queen of Vraja? There is no way to repay you in this world, even with the wealth of Indra.
    Commentary

    Seated with Krsna and Balarama on her lap and embracing them, Yasoda’s mind was completely bewildered, being stunned with bliss and flowing with tears and milk like the Ganga and Yamuna. Of the two seated in front of her, Rohini first spoke. "What can repay that friendship, even if we attained the wealth of Indra." Dvaraka’s wealth was greater, but the expression is for the common man and therefore only mentions Indra’s wealth.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Rohiṇī speaks. How could I not always remember the friendship of you two? Why? That friendship was unceasing. Though we left you and went away, you always served us by sending servants to take news about our well being and always sending presents like ghee and milk gathered personally by you, even though you were far away and now, on the pretext of the pilgrimage, you have come here to see us. In this way your friendship is unceasing. She addressed Yaśodā as queen of Vraja out of respect and prema. She indicated that the wealth of Indra is even greater than that of Dvārakā this because of her forgetfulness. Though one can repay other friends, one cannot forget you since it is impossible to repay you.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    How could I not always remember the friendship of you two? Why? That friendship was unceasing. Though we left you and went away, you always served us by sending servants to take news about our well-being and always sending presents like ghee and milk gathered personally by you, dear to the two brothers, even though you were far away and now, though you still live there, you have come here to stay with us. The reason is that you are the queen of Vraja. By nature you have a soft heart full of affection. Or O queen of Vraja! You are remarkable for your friendship. Or you have more power that Indra or ourselves. O “Why did you not bring my son to my side for so many days?” You are protector of Vraja. If you were to go elsewhere immediately there would be destruction in Vraja. “But do queens remember the suffering people?” Even (api) with more wealth than Indra, you remember. She indicated that the wealth of Indra is even greater than that of Dvārakā because of her forgetfulness. “How can the debt be repaid by offering the two sons to you?” There can be no repayment in this life or in this world by offering your sons or yourselves. We remember your friendship at all times, unable to pay the debt.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Daya (Compassion / Mercy)Vairagya (Detachment / Dispassion)Discriminative WisdomAsteya (Non-stealing / Honesty)Atma Nigraha (Self-control)

    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Some debts cannot be repaid in any currency the world knows — Indra's wealth would still leave the ledger open. Devakī and Rohiṇī acknowledge that Vraja's vātsalya-sevā belongs to a register higher than reciprocation.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.38

    ↗
    ఏతావద‍ృష్టపితరౌ యువయో: స్మ పిత్రో: సమ్ప్రీణనాభ్యుదయపోషణపాలనాని । ప్రాప్యోషతుర్భవతి పక్ష్మ హ యద్వదక్ష్ణో- ర్న్యస్తావకుత్రచభయౌ న సతాం పర: స్వ: ॥ ౩౮ ॥

    Important Words
    • pakṣma — as eyelids of the eyes
      Phrase: pakṣma (eyelash, eyelid) + yadvat (as if) + akṣṇoḥ (of the eyes, genitive dual). The classical Sanskrit simile for protective intimacy. Note: the same word pakṣma will return in verse 39 in the gopīs' famous cursing of the eyelid-maker.
    • samprīṇana-abhyudaya-poṣaṇa-pālanāni — coddling, raising, nourishing, protecting
      Compound: four nouns naming vātsalya's full work. Samprīṇana (caressing), abhyudaya (raising up), poṣaṇa (feeding), pālana (guarding) — the systematic anatomy of parental sevā. Gauḍīyas read this list as a precise definition of vātsalya-rasa's practice.
    • satām — for saintly persons there is no 'other' and 'own'
      Phrase: satām (of the holy) + paraḥ (other) + svaḥ (own) + na (not). The Vaiṣṇava-sādhu does not separate kin from non-kin in sevā. Gauḍīya samadarśana — the equal vision of Bhāgavata 11.2.46's three-tier classification of devotees.
    • bhavati — my good lady
      Honorific second person — 'Your goodness.' The proper Vaiṣṇava address for a senior female devotee. Devakī uses it for Yaśodā even though Yaśodā holds her son. Honour given, not extracted.
    Before these two boys had ever seen Their real parents, you acted as Their parents and gave Them all affectionate care, training, nourishment and protection. They were never afraid, good lady, because you protected Them just as eyelids protect the eyes. Indeed, saintly persons like you never discriminate between outsiders and their own kin.
    Purport

    As Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma had not seen Their parents for two reasons: because of Their exile in Vraja, and also because They are never actually born and therefore have no parents. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī also describes what Devakī thought before speaking this verse: “Alas, because for so long these two sons of mine had you, Yaśodā, as Their guardian and mother, and because They were immersed in such a vast ocean of ecstatic loving dealings with you, now that you are once more before Them They are too distracted to even notice me. Also, you are behaving as if insane and blind with love for Them, showing millions of times more maternal affection than I possess. Thus you simply keep staring at us, your friends, without recognizing us. So let me bring you back to reality on the pretext of some affectionate words.” Then, when Devakī failed to get any response from Yaśodā even after addressing her, Rohiṇī said, “My dear Devakī, it’s impossible just now to rouse her out of this ecstatic trance. We are crying in the wilderness, and her two sons are no less bound up in the ropes of affection for her than she is for Them. So let us now go outside to meet with Pṛthā, Draupadī and the others.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Before these two boys had ever seen Their real parents, you acted as Their parents and gave Them all affectionate care, training, nourishment and protection. They were never afraid, good lady, because you protected Them just as eyelids protect the eyes. Indeed, saintly persons like you never discriminate between outsiders and their own kin.
    Commentary

    "O, after a long time, our two sons, as if meeting their real mother, are bathed in an ocean of bliss. They do not look at us at all though we are present before them. And she also, as if meeting her own son after a long time, is blind with love. She has millions of time more motherly affection than I have. Submerged in an ocean of affection, staring at us, her friends, for a long time, she now does not recognize us. Now I will reveal to her a secret topic through seemingly affectionate words." Thinking in this way, Devaki then spoke in a somewhat loud voice.

    "Having not seen their real parents these two boys received great affection from you two as parents. I placed them safely with you and they lived there without fear, as eyes are protected by eyelids, because great devotees do not distinguish between their own children or other children (nas satam parah svah)." Then not hearing any reply even after a long time, Rohini said, "O friend Devaki, this does not rouse her from her blissful trance. So much for crying in the forest (this has been a waste of time). The two sons also remain bound by ropes of affection for her. We two will go and meet with Kunti, Draupadi and other friends who are very eager to see us outside. Because of having to meet with some many people on this pilgrimage, it is not good for the two of us to stay together with them here." After Rohini said this, Devaki when with her outside.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Devakī speaks this verse. The two boys had not seen their real parents, what to speak of the experience of being cared for by them. The parents only gave birth to them. It is well known (sma) that you became their parents since you satisfied them by giving things that greatly pleased them (samprīṇana), created auspiciousness for them by doing saṁskāras or by making them grow (abhyudaya), nourished them with milk from your breast (poṣaṇa) and protected them by having them rest. Sometimes lālana is seen instead of poṣana. You were able to do these actions for them in the most excellent way (pra—āpya). “This is fine, since she thought that Kṛṣṇa was her own child, but what about Balarāma, whom she knew as Rohiṇī’s child?” Elevated people do not think of “mine” and “others.” This was Devakī’s meaning of the words. She thought, “Yaśodā knows very well that Kṛṣṇa is my son. Therefore such consolation is proper”. But Yaśodā was unaware of anything else because of relishing strong impressions of Kṛṣṇa as her son. She only remembered Kṛṣṇa with great intensity with his promise, “I will come to see my relatives after giving happiness to friends in Mathurā.” (SB 10.45.23) Thus to her, there was another meaning to Devakī’s words. The two boys were offered by you two parents to us Yadus. You experienced their birth which gives happiness to all beings and protected and cared for them, like the eyelid protects the eye, and then, after having attained you, they accepted to live with us without seeing their parents. But there should be no difference. Why would she say this? Still, for the elevated, there is no difference between what is one’s own and belongs to others. , Or, for the elevated, there is no difference between those who are not parents (paraḥ) those who are parents (svaḥ). Since even Balarāma was born in her house the birth of both is referred to in the discussion.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Even though the boys had not seen their parents, what to speak of being cared for by them, from their very birth, you cared for them. You became their parents. Sma indicates that it is well known or indicates surprise. They explain how they are parents. You satisfied them with favorite items (sam-prīṇana) performed the jāta-karma or made them grow (abhyudaya). You made them princes of Vraja and had the demons killed. You nourished them with butter and milk (poṣana) and cared for them, putting them to sleep etc. You did this in an excellent manner (prāpya). Or taking care of the two boys you two, who became creators of their remarkable childhood pastimes (adṛṣṭa-pitarau), lived there. O respected woman (bhavati)! They say this in respect or in joy. Or it means “This is clear (bhavati). They are our children, not yours.” Or how could the two boys be happy in separation from their parents? That is true, good people treat their own and other’s children as their own. O O queen of Vraja! The two boys will definitely suffer without similar pampering. But you do not need to worry. The two boy, not seeing you two parents, being pampered by us just as by you, have lived here somehow without fear. As eyelids are near the eye, they are within our sight. At all times we gaze at them with affection. We cannot leave seeing them for a moment for there is no sense of mine or yours in us, who are your great friends. Your sons are our sons.

    Personal Guidance
    Analogy

    Eyelids do not own the eyes — they exist only to guard them. So Nanda and Yaśodā did not own Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma; they were the eyelids that protected the eyes of the world. The image is perfect: invisible servants of incomparable treasure.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.82.39

    శ్రీశుక ఉవాచ గోప్యశ్చ కృష్ణముపలభ్య చిరాదభీష్టం యత్ప్రేక్షణే ద‍ృశిషు పక్ష్మకృతం శపన్తి । ద‍ృగ్భిర్హృదీకృతమలం పరిరభ్య సర్వా- స్తద్భ‍ావమాపురపి నిత్యయుజాం దురాపమ్ ॥ ౩౯ ॥

    Important Words
    • gopyaḥ — the young cowherd women
      Nominative plural of gopī. Gauḍīyas hold the gopīs as the topmost devotees — mādhurya-rasa personified. Rādhā is their queen; their love is the supreme form of bhakti the entire Bhāgavatam moves toward. Their entrance in this verse is the chapter's climax.
    • pakṣma-kṛtam śapanti — they would curse the maker of (eye)lids
      Phrase: pakṣma (eyelid) + kṛtam (maker) + śapanti (they curse). The famous gopī-complaint — Brahmā made eyelids that blink and break their darśana of Kṛṣṇa. Gauḍīyas read this as mādhurya's intensity: even a half-second's loss of sight is unbearable.
    • tat-bhāvam āpuḥ — they attained ecstatic absorption in Him
      Compound: tat (in Him) + bhāvam (ecstatic absorption) + āpuḥ (they attained, perfect of √āp). Bhāva is Rūpa Gosvāmī's technical term — the second stage above sādhana, the bud of prema. The gopīs reach it instantly upon contact.
    • nitya-yujām durāpam — difficult to attain for those constantly engaged in yoga
      Compound phrase: nitya (constant) + yujām (of yokers, yogīs) + durāpam (hard to attain). The verse's doctrinal claim: gopī-bhāva exceeds yogic samādhi. Gauḍīyas cite this against any view that mādhurya is a kind of mystic absorption — it is its own irreducible category.
    • abhīṣṭam — their object of desire
      Past participle of abhi-√iṣ — that which is desired toward. Kṛṣṇa as the gopīs' abhīṣṭa — not a means to anything else. Gauḍīyas: bhakti's prayojana is Him as Himself, not what He confers.
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: While gazing at their beloved Kṛṣṇa, the young gopīs used to condemn the creator of their eyelids, [which would momentarily block their vision of Him]. Now, seeing Kṛṣṇa again after such a long separation, with their eyes they took Him into their hearts, and there they embraced Him to their full satisfaction. In this way they became totally absorbed in ecstatic meditation on Him, although those who constantly practice mystic yoga find such absorption difficult to achieve.
    Purport

    According to Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī, just then Lord Balarāma saw the gopīs standing a short distance away. Seeing them trembling with eagerness to meet Kṛṣṇa, and apparently ready to give up their lives if they could not, He tactfully decided to get up and involve Himself elsewhere. Then the gopīs attained the state described in the current verse. In referring to the gopīs’ intolerant disrespect of Lord Brahmā, “the creator of eyelids,” Śukadeva Gosvāmī is giving vent to his own subtle jealousy of the gopīs’ favored position. Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī offers an alternate understanding of the phrase nitya-yujām, which may mean “even of the Lord’s principal queens, who tend to be proud of their constant association with Him.” In Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrīla Prabhupāda writes: “Because they had been separated from Kṛṣṇa for so many years, the gopīs, having come along with Nanda Mahārāja and mother Yaśodā, felt intense ecstasy in seeing Kṛṣṇa. No one can even imagine how anxious the gopīs were to see Kṛṣṇa again. As soon as Kṛṣṇa became visible to them, they took Him inside their hearts through their eyes and embraced Him to their full satisfaction. Even though they were embracing Kṛṣṇa only mentally, they became so ecstatic and overwhelmed with joy that for the time being they completely forgot themselves. The ecstatic trance which they achieved simply by mentally embracing Kṛṣṇa is impossible to achieve even for great yogīs constantly engaged in meditation on the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Kṛṣṇa could understand that the gopīs were rapt in ecstasy by embracing Him in their minds, and therefore, since He is present in everyone’s heart, He also reciprocated the embracing from within.”

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: While gazing at their beloved Kṛṣṇa, the young gopīs used to condemn the creator of their eyelids, [which would momentarily block their vision of Him]. Now, seeing Kṛṣṇa again after such a long separation, with their eyes they took Him into their hearts, and there they embraced Him to their full satisfaction. In this way they became totally absorbed in ecstatic meditation on Him, although those who constantly practice mystic yoga find such absorption difficult to achieve.
    Commentary

    When Baladeva, crest jewel of cleverness, saw at a somewhat distant place obstructed from view, the gopis, their hearts bursting in longing, ready to give up their lives if they could not meet him,, he rose and left the room> The gopis then attained an ecstatic state described in this verse. In some text it seems it says rsir uvaca. By designating Sukadeva as rsi, there is indication that he will here reveal the highest truth. The word ca means tu, (but), to distinguish the special position of the gopis from all others. What gopis? Their extraordinary quality is then described: they cursed the creator for making eyelids which obstruct their vision of Krsna.. They could not tolerate the separation from seeing him caused by the moment the eyelid blinked. Though these women had the highest respect for all and any devata, they could curse the chief of all the devas. Such was their intolerance of their separation. This implies anger at Krsna for giving this separation to them (since he is the ultimate creator). Pulling him with their glances, with their eyes as doorways they made him enter their heart, embraced him, and attained rare mahabhava in relation to him (tad bhavam), with at sense of oneness with him "See, I am Krsna." (nityayujam) Or : They attained at state rare even for the best of atma ramas (nitya yujam), the great masters of yoga such as Siva. In this case, there is another hint of anger at Krsna: Krsna will now teach the gopis adhyatma jnana (as in the message sent through Uddhava). Or: they attained mahabhava, rare even to eternal wives (nitya yujam) such as Rukmini.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    With either meaning, Yaśodā did not answer Devakī’s statement. Rather, because she was absorbed in seeing her son after a long time, she did not hear at all and said nothing at all. After she did not speak, Śukadeva the sage endowed with all knowledge (śrī) spoke. He knew the bhāva of the gopīs and Kṛṣṇa. Thus one should firmly believe his words. It should of course be understood that even earlier, for a long time they were thin from experiencing separation from him and were burning up. Śukadeva now describes the arrival of the gopīs who had been brought to Kṛṣṇa’s private quarters by Uddhava. The word ca indicates “not only them but others, the gopīs, also.” Or ca can mean “but.” But more than the others, the gopīs now attained intimately (upa—labhya) Kṛṣṇa whom they desired to see for a long time with complete bhāva (abhi—iṣṭam). Or, they attained for a long period Kṛṣṇa who they held dearer than a thousand lives (abhīṣṭam). Their superior prema is then show. They cursed the creator of eyelids for their eyes because they desired to glance at him without break (pra—īksaṇe). The plural is used in dṛśiṣu to indicate many gopīs with many eyes. Why did the creator not remove the eyelid from the eye? Or because of extreme thirst in prema the plural refers to all the glances. Why were eyelids made for seeing? The long vowel in hṛdī is for metrical reasons. They placed him in their hearts and there embraced him fully (alam), by their free will. All of them (sarvāḥ), though of many different types, filled with pain from long separation, became one with him (tad-bhāvam), forgetting themselves because of an appearance of his clear manifestation. This was rare even for perfected yogīs. Or they had always kept him in their hearts but now they embraced him with their eyes (they gazed at him intensely with the greatest attachment). They then attained the highest limit of prema (tad-bhāvam). This means for a long time they fainted in bliss. This state was rarely attained even by Rukmiṇī and others who were constantly with him (nitya-yujām).
